### NW — Network, back-allocation, capacity, Planning BOM
| ID | Requirement | Closes | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| NW-01 | **Bridge = ITEM LINKS of type bridge (PM → MK)** [gate2r16], on element ID, each with a **weight** in percent — the share of the PM's units on that MK; a PM's bridge weights sum to 100 % in every month: 1:1 and N:1 links weigh 100 %; 1:N weights have no stored source, so they are seeded at equal shares, flagged and listed for the data manager [AD] — a two-axis class per link (B1-B12), with **no plant qualifier** (the 9.9-9.9.1 demand-side qualifier retired: plant-suffixed and `-BR` planning models are merged into their base, their plants carried by alias pins and sourcing rules, CP-07, NW-08 [gate2r17; gate2r19]); the bridge links of merged dEPM IDs collapse into the base planning model's link; dated edges with an as-of guard (GRAFO-01); **YCRANE = 7 MK items, 24 PM items, 24 links of 100 %** [gate2r16]; plant-suffixed supply keys are folded into one item (DF-12), so B5 is one link, no longer 1:N; unmatched links listed with volume; bridge groups (connected PM + MK sets) exposed for the DF-09 alert, the FGNI mapping (CP-02), the frozen-zone shares (CP-08) and the plant / cluster view (UX-05) | DD5, DD11, DM4 | xlsx; vault; corpus; gate2r16; critic r10 |
| NW-02 | §3.3 back-allocation (job 3600) as a live DAG edge: confirmed production at MK × plant × month → confirmed production at LE × region × planning model by each cell's share of the request **at that MK × plant pair** (so a region confirms only from the plants its request reached under the sourcing rule, NW-08), largest remainder; YCRANE split by request % over its 24 bridge links (AC27); a confirmation committed by ops moves the sales-side confirmed inventory and FMS at once (PR-06); the same largest-remainder allocation distributes the frozen-zone order-book default to cells when the order book lacks LE × region × PM, on CP-08's non-circular share basis (pre-default request, else CP-07 split × link weights), never on the defaulted load itself; verified against stored R-instance values since 06/2025 (*tv*) | DD6, DS1, DS3, DM3 | ana050; vault; inputs §4; gate2 OQ-17, OQ-23; gate2r9; gate2r16; critic r8 |
| NW-03 | Semi-finished dependent demand (mandatory routing, 2-month offset, AC6) as ITEM LINKS of type semi-finished [gate2r16] — **the only carrier of a two-step, the `-BA` two-step included** [AD orchestrator, gate2r16; critic r10]: MK → semi-finished item, with a **quantity per finished unit** (> 0; 1 unless Operations says otherwise [AD]), an **offset** in months, a **finishing-plant qualifier** (the plant or plants of the parent's ITEM × PLANT rows whose load triggers the link) and the **supplying ITEM × PLANT row** of the semi-finished item, named by plant ID. The link fires **only on the parent's load at its finishing plant(s), never at other plants**: a finishing load at Cerritos books the semi-finished load at Ganderkesee two months earlier; a finishing load of a NAM key at Niagara books its `-BA` basic assembly at Northfield two months earlier, while the same key built entirely at Northfield books none — one order through two steps, no double count. The plant choice that sends units to the finishing plant comes from CP-07 — for the plan the NAM sourcing rule of the base MK (NW-08) or a routing option — never from a bridge qualifier [gate2r17; AD]; semi-finished key + finishing plant confirmed by the user [gate2r18 MD-Q-20]. It replaces the BA-kit and installation allocation files; `-BR` planning models merged into their base with aliases pinned to Pinecrest do Sul (T12) [gate2r19]. **AC6 in both plants [gate2r14]:** one finished request at Cerritos books the semi-finished load at Ganderkesee two months earlier and the finishing load at Cerritos, and each consumes its own plant's capacity (NW-06); load is checked in Phase 6, capacity consumption in Phase 7 on the same fixture (Plan, "AC6 placement"; split confirmed [gate2r15]). A semi-finished key may carry its own Planning BOM, exploded at its dependent-demand month (NW-07) | DS3, DM8, DD11 | vault; inputs §1; gate2 OQ-20; req; gate2r14; gate2r15; gate2r16; critic r10 |
| NW-04 | **Unified into the Planning BOM (NW-07)** [gate2r14, decided by the orchestrator on the user's behalf]: the elective NAM routing on a configuration-attribute set, colour first (AC7, D-006), is the **routing group** of the MK's Planning BOM, whose options each target an ITEM × PLANT row of the MK; the "pre-assembled at Northfield, finished at Niagara" variant is the routing option to Niagara, whose units then fire the Niagara-qualified semi-finished link of NW-03 — the two-step is never itself a routing option [AD orchestrator, gate2r16; critic r10]; per cell, ordered units follow their real configuration and the remainder the group's rates (NW-07) [AD orchestrator, gate2r14]. One mechanism, so the 9.7.1 rule "only on order-book units; plan units configuration-agnostic" no longer holds. AC7 ("split diversi al variare del mix") is tested by changing a routing rate on a real-data MK (Phase 7) | DS3, DS9 | vault; pbi; gate2r2 OQ-C; gate2r14; req; critic r9; critic r10 |
| NW-05 | Allocation proposal across plants (job 4000) as a live measure, not a day-15 step: order book within its horizon — the Operations Orderbook (the assembly plants' sales orders [gate2r13]) where it has landed, else the sales order book [AD] — beyond it the CP-07 split: routing group where the MK's Planning BOM has one, the cell region's **sourcing rule** (NW-08), else the ITEM × PLANT default shares; override traced (AC8); **when both order books cover a cell, the Operations Orderbook (plant already assigned) places the units across plants and the configured sales Order Book (U1) only supplies their configuration for the components [AD orchestrator, critic r9 note]**. **Sourcing in the proposal [gate2r17; AD]:** only plants allowed for the region are proposed, in priority order; where a plant's capacity check (NW-06, Phase 7) is breached, the proposal offers the excess on the next allowed plant with free capacity — shown, never applied unless a planner or the global supply planner accepts it as an override (CP-07 tier 1, traced); replaces the A instance | DS3, DS5, DS8, DS13, P1, P10, P12 | vault; inputs §4; gate2r9; gate2r12; gate2r13; gate2r17 |
| NW-06 | Capacity = three independent checks per key, plant and month (D-001, AC3), with the key-plant master data on the ITEM × PLANT row (DF-12) [gate2r16]: (1) **hours** — Σ over the MKs of a cluster at a plant of `Average Assembly Hours` of their ITEM × PLANT rows (seeded from the MK attribute on every plant row of the item [AD]) × units, vs the **hour capacity of that plant × cluster × month** [gate2r18 MD-Q-16], the cluster being the ITEM × PLANT row's (DF-12); both master data maintained in the tool by the data manager and Operations [gate2r18 MD-Q-8] (no mask in the vault); hour capacity seeded from `AssemblyCapacity_hours` history by a rule measured in Phase 5 — if the leaf values inside a plant × cluster × month are equal, that value (a repeated cluster figure, as the line-key pattern of A2b suggests *(inference)*), else their sum, flagged [AD]; (2) **production slots** on every leaf key incl. the 73 non-line "(E)" keys such as Basic Assemblies — units vs Cluster average max capacity × Working Days, **both entered by Ops**; line-key rows are max-rollups, never summed with leaves; production on a line key = load "unassigned to a manufacturing key", counted, no slot check [AD]; a missing Working Days value takes the plant's most frequent line value, counted [AD]; the measured history (A2b) is the seed and verification baseline; (3) **installation week-slots** only on the CIT ITEM × PLANT rows (62 today, one per CIT key and plant; master data), counted in DF-13 calendar weeks; production-slot capacity per plant × cluster [gate2r19 OQ-32], seeded by summing the per-key history; week-slot parameters edited by the data manager and Operations [gate2r18 MD-Q-8]; subcontractors = plants under `External` (AC2) [gate2r18 MD-Q-9] | C1-C3, P3, DM6, DD10, DD13 | pbi; corpus; inputs §1; gate2r3 OQ-D'; user slots; measured r7b; gate2r16; gate2r18 |
| NW-07 | **Planning BOM** [UM; gate2r14] — **the configuration part of ITEM LINKS** [gate2r16]: the routing-option and component-option links of one MK (or semi-finished key), grouped into option groups with rate sets, and the phantom-explosion links beneath them; it sits on the same structure as the bridge (NW-01) and the semi-finished links (NW-03), whose links carry fixed weights or quantities and are not options (no group, no regional rate set) [AD]. **Quantity semantics [critic r10]:** weights, rates and attach rates are percentages (0-100 %, 0 % allowed for an option); semi-finished and phantom-explosion links carry a quantity per parent unit (> 0). For each configurable MK, **option groups with rates**. *Two kinds of group [AD orchestrator, gate2r14; critic r9]:* a **routing group** — at most one per MK, its options each target an **ITEM × PLANT row of the same MK, named by plant ID** [critic r10], never a two-step (that is NW-03's semi-finished link, which fires on the units routed to its finishing plant), replacing NW-04 — and **component groups**. A routing option is a component only if it is declared so, as a component of type **feature** — a colour is a feature [gate2r18 MD-Q-19] (component types part / assembly / feature). Each group is either **exclusive** (one choice per unit; rates sum to 100 % on save, else rejected) or, for components only, an **optional attachment** with a standalone attach rate 0-100 % [gate2r14, orchestrator on the user's behalf]. **Single level** in M1 (MK → options; an option is never exploded further; a semi-finished key may have its own Planning BOM, exploded on its dependent demand at its offset month) [gate2r14, orchestrator] — **except that a phantom is transparent** [gate2r16]: an option whose target is a phantom passes its units to the phantom's children × the phantom-explosion link quantities (T12), so the requirement lists only non-phantom components. *Rates:* at **MK × region**, falling back to the **global MK rate** where the region has none; the fallback is **per whole group** — a region rate set overrides the whole group, never single options, and still sums to 100 % [gate2r14; AD orchestrator, gate2r14]; **manual and dated** (effective-from month, history kept) [gate2r14]; once configuration data arrive (U1), the tool **proposes** rates from the observed mix of order lines per MK × region over a stated window (12 months [AD]); the data manager accepts or overrides, every override logged with user, time, value, proposed value and reason [gate2r14; vault]. *Calculation, per cell LE × region × PM × month, mapped to MK through the bridge weights (NW-01) [AD orchestrator, gate2r14]:* (a) **ordered units** = the units of the sales Order Book whose order lines carry a configuration (U1) for that cell; (b) **remainder** = max(0, requested − ordered); if ordered > requested, the ordered units govern, remainder = 0, and the cell is flagged "ordered exceeds requested" and counted (Requested is not changed). (c) **Plant split, once:** ordered units go to the plant of their real routing option; the remainder is split by the routing group's rates; an MK without a routing group takes the CP-07 precedence from tier 3 on (sourcing rule NW-08, then ITEM × PLANT shares, then UNASSIGNED) [critic r12]. The load of a routing option at its plant **is** the units routed there — it is not multiplied by the rate again. (d) **Components, at each plant:** ordered units by their real component options; the remainder's load at the plant × each component group's rate, where a component group may be **conditioned on a routing option** (a rate given that option, applied only to the load routed by that option) — otherwise its rate applies uniformly to all the MK's load at every plant. Example (PB6): red → plant X 30 %, blue → plant Y 70 %, 100 units, nothing ordered → 30 units at X, 70 at Y; red as a component = 30 at X and 0 at Y. Example (PB5): 100 requested, 40 ordered red → Niagara, red rate 30 % → Niagara 40 + 30 % × 60 = 58 units; the plant split and the components follow the same rule. Component requirement (*calc*) keyed **component × plant × region × month**, summable to component × plant × month; stored unrounded (an expected value, so sums stay exact) [AD] and shown with one decimal [gate2r18 UX-Q-22]; a component's month = the MK's production month, with an offset field per BOM line defaulting to 0 [AD; U9]; on the confirmed side (no region, B10) the global group rates apply [AD]. *Order-book horizon:* per cell, the months in which the U1 feed carries ordered units for that cell — no global constant [AD orchestrator, gate2r14]; until U1 delivers there are none, and rates apply everywhere, labelled "rates only". *Scope [gate2r14]:* M1 computes the requirement only — no component capacity, no supply constraint; the requirement is a DAG node keyed like the requested load, so a component capacity node can attach later the way NW-06 attaches to MK load (T10). An MK without a Planning BOM has no component requirement and is counted (all MKs until their Planning BOMs are loaded; **every MK is configurable** [gate2r15], so the tool must hold a Planning BOM for any MK, loaded by file import or entered by hand, and run without one). Rates, groups, routings and phantom links are master data maintained by the data manager, loaded from one global file [gate2r18 MD-Q-21]; **rates are also edited by Operations** [gate2r18 UX-Q-24] — the ops planner, for MKs with an ITEM × PLANT row at its plants [AD]; groups, options and phantom links stay with the data manager [AD]; component codes come from the import, the SAP material as an alias [gate2r18 MD-Q-18]; the component list, the phantoms and initial rates are missing (U9) — no component count is assumed | DS9, DS3, DC14 | gate2r14; gate2r15; gate2r16; vault; xlsx; measured r98; md-spec; gate2r2 OQ-C; critic r9; critic r10 |
| NW-08 | **Regional sourcing rule** [gate2r17; design AD, defaults confirmed gate2r19]: dated master data **ITEM × REGION × PLANT** (DF-12, T13) per MK × sales region (L-2, CP-06) × plant: **allowed** (the row exists), **priority** (1 = preferred; one preferred per MK × region × month), optional **default share** in percent (where any row of an MK × region × month carries a share, the rows' shares sum to 100 % on save, a row without a share counting 0 %). **Use — CP-07 tier 3:** units of an MK in a cell's region and month that no override (alias pins included) or routing rate in force places are split by the rule's shares, else put 100 % on its preferred plant; an MK × region without a rule in force falls to the item-wide ITEM × PLANT shares (tier 4). Example [gate2r17]: LATAM — the LATAM plant priority 1, an EMEA plant priority 2, no shares → LATAM cells load 100 % at the LATAM plant, the EMEA plant an allowed alternative; APAC — NAM, EMEA and LATAM plants with shares 50 / 30 / 20 *(constructed shares for illustration; the user gave the plants, not the shares)* → APAC cells split 50 / 30 / 20. **Checks [AD]:** an allowed plant that is not an ITEM × PLANT row of the MK is accepted and flagged "new key × plant pair" (AC5); a planner override to an allowed plant carries no flag, to a plant not allowed is accepted and flagged "outside sourcing rule"; a routing option outside the allowed plants is flagged "routing outside sourcing"; a rule for a region where no planning model bridged to the MK has an active ITEM × REGION row is warned; nothing is ever rejected except an invalid save (shares ≠ 100 %, two preferred plants). **Interactions:** routing group above it (CP-07; a regional routing rate set is the configuration-driven form of sourcing); CP-08 cell shares and frozen pairs come from the split that includes the rule, and a rule change dated inside a pair's zone does not move its frozen share; NW-05 proposes only allowed plants by priority and offers the next one on a capacity breach; NW-02 back-allocates a confirmation only to the cells whose request reached that plant; UX-05 shows each cell under the plants its split reaches, the region's other allowed plants as 0 % alternatives; the confirmed side has no region (B10) and does not read the rule; a rule change that moves requested load raises a PR-06 alert to ops. **Seed [AD]:** only where history names a plant on the demand side — per base MK × region with units on merged aliases (plant-suffixed and `-BR` planning models, T12) in the Requested history of the last 12 closed months: the alias plants with their share of the region's units, the rest of the region's units on the plants of the MK's item-wide shares that carry no alias, priority by share; no other rule is seeded (Confirmed has no region, B10; `Targeted Plant` differs from supply on 38 of 490 pairs, P11) — the global supply planner and the data manager enter the others (the LATAM and APAC rules of the example included). Maintained by the global supply planner and the data manager [AD, by analogy with gate2r18 MD-Q-10]; dated by month, history kept, logged. Tool-only: not written to the handoff file (no dEPM structure for it is known *(inference)*). **Defaults confirmed [gate2r19 OQ-33]:** (a) routing wins, (b) preferred plant 100 % without shares, (c) a plant outside the list accepted and flagged, (e) seed only where history names a plant. **Seeded rules [gate2r19]:** base model, region NAM → Niagara preferred; base model, region LATAM → Pinecrest do Sul preferred (from the merged `-BR` aliases). **Twin plants [gate2r19; OQ-36]:** a dated pair of plants that build the same MK (Northfield ↔ Niagara first), kept by the global supply planner and the data manager. (1) The planner may move a request share from a plant to its twin in a personal scenario after reading the order book: an ordinary CP-07 override, traced. (2) On each order-book landing the engine re-splits automatically: ordered units whose order line names a plant are placed on that plant and taken off its twin's share in the same cell, the cell total unchanged — e.g. a NAM customer's order built at Northfield lowers the Niagara share and raises Northfield by the ordered units (the ordered-then-remainder rule of CP-07 applied across twins [AD]); inside a frozen zone the frozen share follows CP-08 | DS13, DD11, P12, P5, P11, B6, B7, B8 | gate2r17; gate2r18; vault; xlsx; md |

### PR — Cycle process
| ID | Requirement | Closes | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| PR-01 | **Continuous plan, one monthly review** [gate2r9]: both roles work on the cycle's common plan all month; no day windows in the tool (sales 1-8, ops 10-15, allocation 15, validation 16 stop being gates). m−1 actuals land when available (3rd working day, G5) and replace jobs 3300 / 3100; allocation (4000) and back-allocation (3600) are live calculations (NW-05, NW-02). Once a month the **monthly review** — Portfolio Review (PR-04), Integrated Reconciliation (PR-05), then the decision — publishes `CYCLE MM YYYY R` and `C` (SC-07), hands them to dEPM, closes the cycle node (PR-03) and opens the next cycle's common plan from the published one (replaces job 3000) [AD]; the new cycle's horizon follows CP-01 (one month shorter each month; in January the next FY is added, OQ-4). Review date configurable. P and A are not working instances (R ≈ P, A = C in history, V1, V2) [AD]; they remain as loaded history | DS1, DS2, DS5, DM3, DK4 | inputs §4; gate2 OQ-4, OQ-8; gate2r9; gate2r11; vendors C |
| PR-02 | Every landing of actuals or openings, every order-book update — sales Order Book or Operations Orderbook, any DF-10 channel — and every confirmed change refreshes the common plan (time recorded) and triggers SC-06 on open scenarios; an Operations Orderbook landing also recomputes the frozen-share defaults (CP-08). The sales order book comes through one pluggable source: in M1 the Sales mask channel (as of m−1 end), fed in the pilot by operator-loaded fixtures; a mid-month feed (channel 2 or 3) plugs in when set up (U1), and only then DS4 closes. The Operations Orderbook comes through the Ops mask channel, and through channels 2-3 when U5 / U6 deliver | DS4 (partly), DS7 | inputs §3; gate2 OQ-8; gate2r2 OQ-F; gate2r6; gate2r9; gate2r12 |
| PR-03 | At the monthly review the tool-side cycle node and its published versions become read-only and are snapshotted (QC-03); writes rejected (AC20, STAT_03) | DS2 | corpus; inputs §4; vendors D |
| PR-04 | **Portfolio Review**, gate `PortfolioApproved`, held in the monthly review (AC21), no fixed days [UM]. It reviews (a) the cycle's SOP / EOP and Region Model Assignment changes (ITEM × REGION) and (b) **new models to create and enter into the plan** [gate2r18 UX-Q-5]: a new planning model and / or MK prepared as a **proposed bundle** — ITEM rows with classification and hierarchies, ITEM × REGION rows with SOP, bridge links, ITEM × PLANT rows with default shares and sourcing rules (global supply planner or data manager), optionally a Planning BOM — validated like any master data (DF-11) but not read by the engine while proposed [AD]. On approval the bundle becomes active from its valid-from month: the planning model's cells for its assigned regions appear in the sales mask from SOP on, empty, and its load flows like any item's; a rejected bundle stays logged [AD] | DS12, S11 | gate G3; gate2r9; gate2r18; md; inputs §1; ana050 §3.3 |
| PR-05 | Integrated Reconciliation agenda of the monthly review (the cockpit agenda), fed continuously: open PR-06 alerts, engine gaps above threshold in units and value, accuracy gaps (AC22), open DF-09 alerts, missing openings, plants that did not report FGNI, **frozen-zone exceptions** (CP-08) with reason and author, Requested load on an UNASSIGNED plant (CP-07 tier 5) — inside a frozen window flagged first —, plants without a region, proposed new-model bundles awaiting the Portfolio Review, overrides flagged "outside sourcing rule" or "routing outside sourcing", MK × region pairs with demand and no sourcing rule by volume, and cells without price or FX rate for the value gap [UM; gate2r12; gate2r17; gate2r18] | — | gate G3; gate2r9; gate2r12; critic r7; critic r10 |
| PR-06 | **Cross-function alerts on confirm** [gate2r9]: a confirmed change that moves the requested-vs-confirmed gap at MK × plant × month, a capacity check (NW-06) or the confirmed inventory / FMS at LE × region × PM raises an alert for the other role — sales → ops on new or changed requested load, frozen-zone exceptions included; ops → sales on shortfall (confirmed < requested) or a changed confirmation, seen on the back-allocated confirmed inventory and FMS (NW-02, CP-03), and on an Operations Orderbook landing that moves the frozen-share default of Requested [AD]. Routing by data ownership and write scope (sales: Sales / Invoices, Order Book, Requested, Financial, IC; ops: Confirmed, Working Days, capacity, Operations Orderbook, FGNI) [AD]; a master-data change that moves requested load across plants — a sourcing rule, a default plant share, a routing rate — alerts ops with the same threshold [AD]; view-only roles see alerts inside their read scope; threshold ≥ 1 unit on the gap delta, configurable [AD]. An alert names the causing change, its author and the affected cells, can open a scenario to resolve it, and feeds PR-05; the receiving role marks it "seen" with an optional note, and the engine closes it when its gap closes or the monthly review records it — no explicit "resolved" act [gate2r18 UX-Q-16]. Replaces the wait for the day-10 handover and the Excel back-allocation. Vendor alert mechanics are not public [vendors C] | DS1, DS3, DS4 | vendors C; gate2r9; gate2r12; inputs §4 |

| QC-06 | Ops accuracy at plant × MK vs C: STAT_OPS_DIRECT and STAT_OPS_NET | DP6 | baseline 4; gate2 OQ-14 |
| QC-07 | FVA STAT → published R per cycle (P only from loaded history, V1): in M1 (pilot) on replayed history cycles; on live cycles from M2 | DP5, DS5 | vault; gate2 OQ-2; gate2r6; gate2r9 |
| QC-08 | KPI tables readable by Power BI without parsing version names (the only tool output Power BI reads in M1); inventory / FMS labelled requested or confirmed | DK3 | pbi |

Diagrams chosen by the user (AC26) [PREF] | DM6 | corpus; gate2r10 |
| UX-03 | **Authenticated access and role-based scopes in M1** [UM; gate2r10]: every session logs in; roles per T11 — write roles (sales planner, ops planner, **global supply planner** [gate2r18 MD-Q-10], approver, pilot operator, data manager) and three view-only roles: Manager (own plant or own LE), Regional Head (sales and ops of own region), Corporate function (all). **Read scopes** — axes plant, LE, region (L-2), **Product Division** [gate2r18 MD-Q-6], all; a grant may combine region and Product Division as today's Power BI RLS does (a row is visible if it matches both axes of one grant; visible if any grant matches) [AD] — apply to the common plan, published official versions, loaded references and shared-for-review scenarios; personal scenarios are visible only to their author. **Write scopes** limit planners to their assigned LE(s) or plant(s) [AD]. **Enforcement is server-side:** every engine read and write takes the authenticated principal and filters rows before any value leaves the engine — compare, KPIs, alerts and roll-ups included (FMS and gaps on in-scope sums); the client only renders; a call without a valid principal is rejected. A region scope covers sales rows of that region, requested load carrying that region, and ops rows of the plants assigned to that region in master data, seeded from the plant dimension's `Region` attribute [md] (confirmed production has no region, B10); the 5 plants without a region (OEM, OEM-Delta, CIS External Installation, Dummy Plant, `SEA (E)`) are a data-quality case: counted, on the PR-05 agenda, and visible only to `all` scopes until the data manager assigns them [AD]. **Precedent [md]:** today the Power BI Sales model already filters rows per user email by Product Division and Region (A6), the Ops model not at all; the tool's scopes extend this to plant and LE and to the ops side. **Product Division scope [gate2r18 MD-Q-6]:** a sales row's division is its planning model's `Product Division`; an ops row's divisions are those of the planning models bridged to its MK, visible if any matches [AD]; rows whose item has no Product Division (278 of 1,888 product rows, mostly "(E)" members [md]) are visible only to grants without a division restriction, counted [AD]; write scopes stay LE / plant [AD]. **Role switch [gate2r18 UX-Q-17]:** a user holding several roles switches the active role without signing out; every read and write after the switch uses the active role's scopes, the log records the active role, personal scenarios belong to the user, not the role [AD]. The plant / cluster view of the sales plan (UX-05) is a sales read: it is scoped by LE, region and Product Division like every sales row, not by plant [AD]. Users, roles, scopes and plant → region assignments are master data kept by the data manager; identity source = tool-local accounts with salted password hashes and a data-manager-entered user list [gate2r18, U7 default accepted]. **M1 shape:** one server process owns the store; many authenticated readers; writes serialized through the server, one writer at a time in the pilot; simultaneous multi-writer editing and the web client are M2 | DC10 | gate2r10; corpus (API-01, AC25); inputs §4 ("Check Actuals_CK"); md; gate2r18 |

| UX-05 | **Sales plan by plant and cluster, and the frozen zone shown to sales (S-04)** [gate2r16; S-04 display approved]. *Plant and cluster view:* the sales planning mask filters and sorts by producing plant and by cluster, read through PM → bridge links → the MK's ITEM × PLANT rows (DF-12); a cell (LE × region × PM × month) appears under every plant its MK × plant shares reach, with that share — CP-08's split (pre-default request, else CP-07 split × bridge weights) —, so the rows under all plants plus "(unassigned plant)" add up to the cell [AD for the unassigned row]; **cluster = the planning model's Assembly Cluster** [gate2r17 OQ-29], from the one cluster list shared with the capacity side [gate2r18 MD-Q-1]; a planning model without one shows under "(no cluster)" [AD]; the plant filter, not the cluster filter, separates the shares of a multi-plant planning model. Requested shows the share's units; Sales / Invoices, the Order Intake forecast and actuals are shown with the same share and are read-only under a plant or cluster filter, because the engine splits only Requested by plant [AD]. Shares are shown unrounded; a rounded display rounds by largest remainder per cell so the totals still add up [AD]. *Frozen-zone display per cell × month:* three states — **open** (no share frozen), **frozen** (every non-zero share frozen), **partially frozen** "k of n plants" (e.g. "1 of 2 plants"; n = plants with a non-zero share or a frozen default in that month [AD]); a cell expands into per-plant sub-rows (plant, MK where the PM links to several, share %, units, the frozen months of that plant), the cell region's preferred plant marked and its other allowed plants listed at 0 % as alternatives (NW-08) [AD]; a tooltip per plant gives its zone length, the month its zone ends, the default source (Operations Orderbook, or "order book missing") and any exception; a header band per plant above the month columns shows where each plant's zone ends; with a plant filter the cell shows only that plant's share and zone state. *Edits follow CP-08:* an edit of the cell total spreads over the open shares only; if every share is frozen, or the planner edits a frozen sub-row, the edit is an exception with a reason (S-17); an "(unassigned plant)" sub-row has no zone. Engine read printed by the CLI from Phase 6 (plant / cluster) and Phase 7 (frozen states); S-04 in the app in Phase 12 | DS11 | gate2r16; inputs §1; xlsx; vault; md; critic r7 |
| RG-01 | AC10-AC16 and the 187-test suite green in every phase, except deliberate rewrites listed here or in the phase that makes them, with reason. Listed now: `tests/test_scenario_ac13_promotion.py::test_write_to_an_existing_instance_name_is_rejected` (expects `DuplicateVersionError` on an existing `version_name` [critic r5]) becomes "duplicate **identity** rejected" in Phase 5 and gains "publish under an existing official name beside the loaded reference succeeds; a re-publish supersedes, logged" in Phase 9 (SC-07). Any other test touched by the identity refactor (e.g. `test_scenario_ac16_conflict.py`, `test_seed_ac16_snapshot.py`, which read `version_name` [code r9.3]) keeps its assertion and only changes its lookup to identity; tests that read `item.region`, `item.is_active`, `production_eligibility` or `routing` (listed by the Phase 5 wave-1 grep, not yet counted) keep their assertion and change only their lookup to the DF-12 structures; tests that call the engine get a test principal with full scope once UX-03 lands | regression | code; critic r5; code r9.3; schema |

Count: CP 9 + SC 8 + DF 14 + NW 8 + PR 6 + QC 9 + UX/RG 6 = **60**.

## To-be

**T1 Shape.**

 Common plan of the cycle (one per cycle, both roles; horizon = rest of current FY + next FY, current-FY actuals alongside)

   facts (actuals, openings, order books) land directly; confirmed changes visible to sales and ops at once; alerts to the other role (PR-06)
                                                                            ▼
 Concurrent DAG (set-based, incremental):
   opening = Total (Financial + IC) + FGNI (plants) ─► inventory row: end(m−1) − external Sales/Invoices + production [requested | confirmed] ─► FMS (LE × region × PM × month; roll-ups by the same formula; window ends at next FY end)

        semi-finished load (−2 months, supplying plant; only on load at the link's finishing plant) ─┘

 chain read view (UX-04): PM → MK → plant → semi-finished → components → overrides, computed from the item links
 monthly review: Portfolio Review · Integrated Reconciliation · decide ─► publish `CYCLE MM YYYY R` / `C` (own identity) + dEPM handoff (dEPM IDs through the aliases) ─► next cycle
 ─ ─ training / verification store (DWH extracts, read-only) ─► calibrated profiles · seeded shares · STAT · fixtures · KPI harness on U ─► KPI tables ─► Power BI

**T2 Decisions that shape it.**
1. **Two acts: confirm a change, publish the month.** A scenario is the unit of work; personal until shared, shared until confirmed. Confirm commits a change into the cycle's common plan, visible to both roles at once [gate2r9]; R-001 Step B item 1 moves into M1 (SC-01) by ADR-001 amendment [corpus]. Once a month the common plan is frozen and published as `CYCLE MM YYYY R` and `C` [gate2r7; gate2r9], each with its own identity beside the loaded reference of the same name (DF-03), and handed to dEPM by file; the official version reaches Power BI through dEPM and the DWH. No name outside the tool is rewritten in M1; scenarios never reach Power BI, which dissolves the `MID(text,10,4)` naming conflict [pbi]. In-place publish into dEPM is the M2 target (R-001 Step B item 2) [gate2r2 OQ-H].
2. **Planner inputs drive the engine; history only trains and verifies it** [gate2r5]. Inputs land through three channels on one contract [gate2r6]; in the pilot, real history arrives as fixtures through channel 1. The engine is rebuilt set-based and proven at real size in slice 1 by pushing real history through the input layer as fixtures (DC4: 14,397 queries for 3,492 pairs). Wave 1 counts pairs and measures latency; if CP-05 is missed, Phase 5 stops and reports; no partition fallback [corpus].
3. **Confirmed production is read at the grain it is entered.** No region at MK × plant (B10); the regional and product-grain view comes through the back-allocation edge (Phase 6). No region invented.
4. **Plants come from evidence and from rules the planners keep, never from a fallback, and an unlisted plant is flagged, not refused.** Precedence: override (alias pins of merged dEPM IDs included), routing group, the regional sourcing rule of the MK × region, master split — ITEM × PLANT default shares for a multi-plant MK, the single row for a single-plant MK, the product `Plant` attribute read only there and as seed fallback —, `UNASSIGNED` [critic r9; critic r10; gate2r17; AD orchestrator, gate2r14; AD orchestrator, gate2r16]. A plant outside an MK's ITEM × PLANT rows is accepted and flagged (AC5 realigned, CP-07) [gate2r14]. Old Phase 6 (`ANY_SCOPE`) discarded (DM2).
5. **Accuracy is a control, not a gate,** scored on DWH history over U.
6. **Two slot concepts, never merged.** Production slots and hours on every key (Basic Assemblies included); installation week-slots only on CIT keys [user slots; gate2r3 OQ-D']. D-001 holds.
7. **Inventory and FMS are consequences, the opening an input.** No target; the m−1 actual lands at LE × region × PM through any channel as Financial + IC = Total, plus the FG that assembly plants hold and have not yet invoiced, reported by the plants [gate2r12] — so units produced and not invoiced stop falling between entities (DD9), and since IC leaves those units out they are counted once [gate2r13]; a coarser channel 2-3 arrival is spread on the cycle's own plan (CP-02); the row rolls forward on requested or confirmed production by switch and is depleted only by external invoices; FMS follows at the same grain with dEPM's logic, live instead of after job 3100 (CP-03).
8. **Sales and ops actuals meet through the bridge, on one stock identity.** Sales actuals stay at PM, ops actuals at MK [gate2r8]; the production alert compares them per bridge group × month (DF-09, NW-01), so neither side is rolled up to product line; the sales side uses the inventory row's own identity (external depletion), and intra-group invoices net out because every LE of the group is summed [critic r7].
9. **Time gates become state gates** [gate2r9; vendors C]. Visibility follows the level of a change (personal / shared / confirmed), not the day of the month; the requested-vs-confirmed and capacity gaps raise alerts to the other role the moment a change is confirmed (PR-06); only the monthly review keeps a date. P and A stop being working instances.
10. **Who sees what is decided in the engine, not in the screen** [gate2r10]. One server process owns the store; every read and write carries the logged-in principal and is filtered by its scope before values leave the engine, so the Windows client of M1 and the web client of M2 share the same rules.
11. **The horizon follows the fiscal year, not a rolling window** [gate2r11]. Every node, mask and forward window ends at the end of the next FY; the current FY's closed months show actuals. FY = calendar year [gate2r12].
12. **Inside the frozen zone the order book plans, per MK × plant, and the planner excepts** [gate2r12; critic r7]. The zone is not a lock and is not a property of a planning-model cell: each MK × plant pair — an ITEM × PLANT row — has its own zone, and only the share of the cell that flows to a frozen pair follows the Operations Orderbook by its latest dates, so late orders move by themselves (DS7) while the rest of the cell stays the planner's. Which part of a cell flows to which pair is read from the planner's pre-default request, else from the CP-07 split, never from the defaulted load [critic r8]. A human change of a frozen share is a logged exception with a reason that the monthly review sees. Operations owns its order book — the assembly plants' sales orders [gate2r13] — at plant × MK (DS8); it has no history anywhere [gate2r18], so the pilot proves the mechanism on a labelled proxy and constructed late orders; zone lengths are given per MK × plant [gate2r18 MD-Q-7]; the ops planner reads only confirmed Requested. Sales sees the same shares per plant, in three states (UX-05) [gate2r16].
13. **Configuration is planned statistically per MK, never per unit** [gate2r14]. The Planning BOM gives each configurable MK at most one routing group, which splits the MK load across plants exactly once, and component groups, which apply to the load at each plant — conditioned on a routing option where declared, so a plant never receives a component its routing excludes [AD orchestrator, gate2r14; critic r9]. Per cell, ordered units follow their real configuration (U1) and only the remainder follows rates, for plants and components alike. M1 stops at the requirement; the requirement node is keyed like the MK load, so component capacity attaches later without redesign. Storage stays normalised — one ITEM table and its dated ITEM × PLANT, ITEM × REGION and ITEM LINKS rows (DF-12) — and the whole chain is read through one computed view (UX-04), not a wide table.
14. **One item master, five structures** [gate2r16; gate2r17]. Everything that is planned, produced, used or passed through is an ITEM, told apart by classification; where it is made is ITEM × PLANT, where and when it is sold is ITEM × REGION, which plants may supply which region is ITEM × REGION × PLANT, and every relation between items — bridge, semi-finished, routing and component options, phantom explosion — is a typed dated ITEM LINK. The special cases of today's bridge stop being naming conventions: a plant suffix becomes an ITEM × PLANT row on the supply side and an alias of (planning model, plant) plus a sourcing rule on the demand side [gate2r17], an aggregating key becomes several links onto one item, a two-step a plant-qualified semi-finished link with an offset, a phantom a transparent node. Each relation has exactly one carrier: a plant split comes from one place (CP-07), a two-step from one link type (NW-03) [critic r10]. The code already holds most of it (A3); the deltas are named in the Plan.
15. **Where a region buys is a dated rule, not a model name** [gate2r17]. A region's allowed plants, its preferred plant and optional shares are master data per MK × region, kept by the global supply planner and the data manager; the sales row stays one planning model per region, and the plant choice moves from the ID into the rule. Configuration still wins where a routing rate applies (a colour that only one plant paints); the rule fills the rest before the item-wide shares; frozen pairs, back-allocation and the allocation proposal all read the split the rule produced, so one rule moves load, zone and confirmation consistently.
16. **One cluster, one calendar, one currency rule** [gate2r18]. The cluster is one value list read by the sales filter (Assembly Cluster on the planning model) and by the hour capacity (cluster on ITEM × PLANT, at plant × cluster); time is one day-grain calendar with ISO weeks and months; money is shown in the entity's currency and summed only in EUR.

Spread bases are never *tv*: SC-02 uses the scenario's current values (the Order Intake fallback uses the plan Sales / Invoices of the same cells), CP-02 the cycle's own plan inputs, CP-08 the pre-default request or the CP-07 split, all in the operational store (the default plant shares and the sourcing rules are master data seeded once from history, DF-01).

**Channels in M1 [AD].** Channel 1 and the landing contract are built; channels 2 (ERP extract) and 3 (Snowflake) are specified as adapters on the contract and proven with test doubles (a second source lands the same values with the same result), not built against real systems. Reasons: M1 is a pilot whose inputs are history fixtures, which arrive as files through channel 1; no ERP extract layout or Snowflake object is in the evidence, so a real adapter now would be designed on invented facts; the contract is what M2 live use needs, and it is what makes a later adapter cheap. Real adapters are built when U5 / U6 deliver, at the latest before M2 live use. Plan figures (Sales / Invoices, Order Intake forecast, Requested, Confirmed, capacity) are planner judgement, so ch1 only; actuals, openings, FGNI and both order books may come from any channel *(inference)*. A Snowflake link needs network access; the single-PC mode runs offline, the link is used when reachable (U6). "Immediate" holds inside the tool; an ERP or Snowflake fact is visible only once it lands [vendors C].

Actuals grain mapping: the user's LE × region × planning model ("as we do now") for the opening [gate2r5; gate2r12] and for all sales actuals [gate2r8] maps to the vault as LE = Sales Division (legal sales entity → Company Legal Name; 6 LNs have several codes, E8), region = the mask filter, planning model = the row. **Contradiction kept visible:** the UM's Input Actuals mask holds Invoiced, Order Intake, Orderbook and Inventory per Division × product line, with region only a filter [inputs §1, §2]; the user corrects it to planning model, which is also the grain of the DWH sales facts and of stored FMS [pbi; fms]. The tool takes the user's grain. The coarse-opening spread stays only as a fallback for channels 2-3, whose grains are unknown (U5, U6); the mask channel and the Phase 5 fixtures land at PM. The `DSALES_Product` input grain of OQ-13 and the mask's planning-model rows are treated as one key where 1:1 (~1,661 products vs 1,659 PMs *(inference)*; mapping counted at seed).

**T4 Concurrent engine.** Source nodes = the DF-10 inputs. Every node is indexed over the cycle's horizon (CP-01): closed current-FY months hold actuals, open months to the end of the next FY hold plan. Opening node = Total + FGNI; inventory row with switch, depleted by external Sales / Invoices; FMS node at LE × region × PM × month with forward-window edges up to the horizon edge, roll-ups by the same formula (CP-03). The Order Intake forecast is a source node with no outgoing edge in M1. Requested load keyed `(MK, plant, region, month)` — `(MK, plant)` is an ITEM × PLANT row; confirmed keyed `(MK, plant, month)`; gap on requested summed over region. Master data are read as of the plan month of the cell (EDR-8 [md-spec]): the bridge weights, the ITEM × PLANT attributes and shares, the ITEM × REGION rows and the sourcing rules valid in that month; months and weeks come from the DF-13 calendar. Dependent demand: a semi-finished node per link reads the parent's load only at the link's finishing plant(s) and writes the supplying row's load at month − offset × quantity per unit. Capacity (Phase 7): hours per plant × cluster (Σ AAH × units of the cluster's MKs vs the plant × cluster hour capacity) and production slots on every leaf key from Ops-entered capacity × working days; line-key rows treated as rollups; installation week-slots on the CIT ITEM × PLANT rows. Frozen zone (CP-08): per ITEM × PLANT row on Ops-entered working days; a Requested cell is held as its MK × plant shares, whose split is stored from the pre-default request (else derived from the CP-07 split × link weights) so the default never feeds its own split; inside a pair's zone, a default node from the Operations Orderbook sets that pair's share, with exceptions as an overlay that wins over the default; the UX-05 sales view reads these shares and zone states, it computes nothing of its own. **Planning BOM (Phase 7):** per cell, an ordered / remainder node (ordered units from the configured Order Book, remainder = max(0, requested − ordered), flag when ordered exceeds requested) feeds the plant split (routing group, once) and a component-requirement node keyed `(component, plant, region, month)`, which reads the finished and semi-finished load at each plant, the component-group rates in force for that MK × region × month (region group else global), and their routing-option conditions; a phantom target is passed through to its children by the phantom-explosion quantities before the node writes, so no phantom key ever exists in the node; a rate edit, a Requested edit or an order landing recomputes it incrementally like any node. AC10-AC12 on real-size data. The same DAG computes the common plan and every scenario; a confirm is an overlay merge followed by an incremental recompute, so the other role reads the new values on its next read. The scope filter sits in the engine's read path, after calculation and before output, so values are computed once and only their exposure depends on the principal.

**T5 Scenarios.** `branch()` takes one parent node (the common plan or a scenario). Edits carry the input key and are checked against the author's write scope, the open months and the frozen zone (an edit that changes a frozen share carries a reason); aggregate edits spread by SC-02 (openings by the CP-02 basis; Requested over open shares only; an all-zero Order Intake aggregate on plan Sales / Invoices). `compare()` returns every measure, inventory and FMS on one switch, FMS of a group recomputed by the CP-03 formula, all inside the reader's scope. Rebase replays the log. Share for review freezes content; confirm merges into the common plan after the conflict check and logs approver, user, time, reason, source. Monthly publish freezes the common plan into `CYCLE MM YYYY R` / `C` (identity = cycle, instance, origin, sequence; an earlier publish of the cycle superseded) and writes the handoff file. Lifecycle (personal / shared for review / confirmed / discarded) and cycle are independent. Planning BOM rates, default plant shares, sourcing rules, FX rates and all item-master rows are master data, not scenario data, in M1 [AD]: a what-if on rates is an M2 extension *(design rationale: rates are dated master data maintained by one owner)*.

**T6 Network and capacity.** Two-axis bridge on dated ITEM LINKS with weights and bridge groups; back-allocation edge moves the confirmed inventory row, confirmed FMS and the regional gap the moment ops confirms; its largest-remainder allocation also distributes the frozen-zone order-book default to cells, on CP-08's non-circular share basis; the production alert and the FGNI mapping read the bridge groups; dependent demand replaces the BA-kit and installation files and books load in both plants of a mandatory routing (AC6), firing only on the parent's load at the link's finishing plant; the Planning BOM's routing group splits each cell's MK load across plants once — ordered units by their real configuration, the remainder by rates — replacing the order-book-only elective routing, and its component groups explode the load at each plant into component requirements, conditioned on the routing option where declared, phantoms passed through; bridge, semi-finished, routing, component and phantom links are one dated structure (DF-12); allocation proposal live, on the Operations Orderbook within its horizon and on allowed plants by priority beyond it (NW-05, NW-08); back-allocation per MK × plant pair, so a region confirms only from the plants its request reached; three independent capacity checks on the ITEM × PLANT master data.

**T7 Process.** One common plan per cycle, worked continuously by both roles; three visibility levels (SC-05); confirm commits a change and raises cross-function alerts (PR-06); facts land directly and rebase open scenarios (PR-02) and raise DF-09 alerts; an Operations Orderbook landing moves the frozen-share defaults; missing openings, unreported FGNI, frozen-zone exceptions, UNASSIGNED load, plants without region, sourcing flags, new-model bundles and value-gap cells without price or FX flagged on the agenda; the Portfolio Review approves new models into the plan (PR-04); one monthly review decides and publishes R and C (PR-01, SC-07), freezes the cycle node (PR-03) and opens the next cycle with a horizon one month shorter, or extended by the next FY in January. **What the pilot demonstrates [AD]:** with test accounts per role on one server, a change confirmed in a sales-planner session appears at once in an ops-planner session as requested load, gap and alert; a confirmation in the ops session appears at once in the sales session as confirmed inventory, FMS and shortfall alert; an Operations Orderbook landing with a moved order date moves the frozen share of Requested and the sales inventory, and the sales session sees the cell turn partially frozen at that plant; a personal scenario stays invisible to every other session until confirmed; a sourcing-rule change by the global supply planner moves the LATAM load to the LATAM plant and alerts ops; a user switches role without signing out and reads with the new scope; Manager, Regional Head and Corporate sessions see only their scope, read-only; a monthly review is replayed on history. Not demonstrated: simultaneous writers, notification delivery, live use (M2).

**T8 Quality control.** On the training / verification store only: snapshot store, KPI harness, human values on U, STAT, STAT_OPS_DIRECT / NET, accuracy report, KPI tables.

**T9 Surfaces.** Slice 1: CLI (import mask-layout input files, enter the opening — Financial, IC, FGNI —, create scenario, apply an edit file, set the switch, print the consequence diff over the horizon with current-FY actuals, write a dry-run handoff file); user checks with a UAT checklist. Phase 6: CLI print of the sales plan by plant and cluster (UX-05). Phase 7: CLI print of the chain (UX-04) for one PM and of the three-state frozen cells (UX-05). Phase 8-9: CLI calls carry a test principal, so role views, scopes and the alert inbox are testable. Final slice: Windows desktop app with login, input masks with "i" definitions (opening-inventory mask S-06 with its Financial / IC / FGNI pop-up, sales mask S-04 with the Order Intake forecast, the plant and cluster filters and the per-plant frozen-zone display, ops mask with Operations Orderbook S-07, exception dialog S-17), role views, alert inbox, monthly review view, the chain read view (UX-04) and the master-data maintenance masks per the DF-11 spec (item master, sourcing rules, calendar, FX rates, Order Intake level, new-model bundles and Planning BOM included), role switch in the header, on the tool server and its API.

**T10 Path to M2 (dEPM replacement).** M2 makes the tool the system of record: simultaneous multi-writer input in the masks, alert notification, the **web app** on the same API [gate2r10], in-place publish into dEPM's official version (R-001 Step B items 2-3, DAX `version_name` redesign), cycle versions exported by the tool, dEPM retired. M1 does not block it: login, principals and scopes already sit in the engine (UX-03), so a web client adds a front end, not a security model; inputs, edits, shares, confirms and publishes carry user, time, reason, approver, source (SC-05, DF-10); every channel is an adapter on one landing contract (DF-10); scenario, common plan and published version are distinct (SC-07); a published version already carries the official name; the handoff file is the single boundary adapter; the order-book sources are pluggable (PR-02); version identity is separate from its name (DF-03); master data follow one specification (DF-11) on one item master whose dEPM IDs are aliases (DF-12), so retiring dEPM drops aliases, not keys; the component requirement is a keyed DAG node, so component capacity (on a future ITEM × PLANT or supplier structure), supply constraints and a multi-level Planning BOM attach to it in M2+ (NW-07) *(design rationale)*. **M1 is a pilot** [gate2r6]: one operator loads history fixtures, no planner keys anything twice. **Double entry is the M2 concern:** live use makes the tool the input point beside dEPM until dEPM is retired; channels 2-3 remove the keying of actuals, openings and order books. Accuracy QC and back-tests keep using DWH history in M1 and M2. Vendor principles are the reference [vendors D; vendors C]; the mechanisms are ours to design [gate2r4]: audit content and retention, concurrent-edit rule on shared scenarios, alert thresholds and routing.

**T11 Roles and access (UX-03) [gate2r10; gate2r18; scopes and write rights per role are AD unless tagged].** A user may hold several roles and switches the active one without signing out [gate2r18 UX-Q-17]; any read scope may add a Product Division restriction [gate2r18 MD-Q-6]. "Operations" in the user's answers = the ops planner within its plants [AD; gate2r19].

| Role | Writes | Read scope |
|---|---|---|
| Sales planner | sales plan figures (Order Intake forecast and frozen-share exceptions with reason included), sales actuals and opening Financial / IC for assigned LE(s); personal scenarios; confirm own-role changes | all (the common plan is shared by design) |
| Ops planner | ops plan figures, Working Days, cluster average max capacity, Operations Orderbook, FGNI and Produced Units for assigned plant(s); hour capacity at plant × cluster, Average Assembly Hours and week-slot parameters of its plants [gate2r18 MD-Q-8]; Planning BOM rates of MKs produced at its plants [gate2r18 UX-Q-24; scope AD]; personal scenarios; confirm own-role changes | all |
| Global supply planner [gate2r18 MD-Q-10] | ITEM × PLANT rows of every MK — create, end-date, default plant shares — with the data manager [gate2r18]; regional sourcing rules (NW-08) [AD]; plant overrides and acceptance of allocation proposals on any plant in personal scenarios, confirmed as own-role changes [AD]; parts of new-model bundles (plants, shares, sourcing) [AD]; Confirmed production, capacity parameters and frozen-zone lengths on every plant [gate2r19 OQ-34]; no sales figures [AD] | all |
| Approver | confirm from review; monthly publish | all |
| Pilot operator | fixtures and facts through the channels; dry-run handoff files | all |
| Data manager | master data per DF-11, including the **item master** (DF-12: ITEM, ITEM × PLANT with its default plant shares, ITEM × REGION, sourcing rules, ITEM LINKS — bridge weights, semi-finished links with their plant qualifiers, the **Planning BOM** with routing and component groups, options, rates, conditions and phantom links, one global import file; accepts or overrides rate proposals), the calendar, FX rates monthly, the Order Intake level, new-model bundles, users, roles, scopes, plant → region, frozen-zone lengths, hour capacity [AD; gate2r18] | all |
| Manager (view only) | — | own plant (ops rows of that plant) or own LE (sales rows of that LE) (± Product Division) |
| Regional Head (view only) | — | sales and ops rows of own region (ops via plants assigned to the region) (± Product Division) |
| Corporate function (view only) | — | all (± Product Division) |

**T12 Item master (DF-12) [gate2r16].**

| Structure | Key | Holds | Dated | Replaces (schema [schema]; spec [md-spec]) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ITEM | item key; origin + element ID unique (DF-04) | element ID, caption, classification, member type, product hierarchy (planning models), manufacturing hierarchy (MKs, semi-finished), descriptive attributes (Targeted Plant, product `Plant`, Assembly Cluster (read by the sales cluster filter [gate2r17]), WSC group …), valid from / to | validity; descriptive attributes per EDR-7 | `item` without `region`, `is_active` and the MK capacity attributes; D-04, D-05, D-13 |
| ITEM × PLANT | item × plant | default plant share, cluster (= Manufacturing Category = Assembly Cluster [gate2r18]), Average Assembly Hours, week-slot parameters, slot-equivalence factor, frozen-zone length, other MK-specific attributes; the row's existence = the item is produced at the plant | Y, by month (EDR-1…6) | `production_eligibility`, `item.average_assembly_hours`, `item.slot_equivalence_factor`, the plants of `routing`; L-03, P-02…P-05 |
| ITEM × REGION | item × region | Region Model Assignment (active, phase-in month, phase-out month), SOP / EOP | Y, by month | `item.region`, `item.is_active`; L-06, P-01 |
| ITEM × REGION × PLANT (sourcing) [gate2r17] | MK × region (L-2) × plant | allowed, priority (1 = preferred), optional default share (NW-08) | Y, by month | — (new; the plant choice encoded today in demand IDs, DS13) |
| ITEM LINKS | link ID; from item, to item (or to an ITEM × PLANT row), type | type, percentage (weights, rates) or quantity per parent unit (semi-finished, phantom), offset, class, plant qualifiers, group and condition for option links | Y, by month, as-of guard | `item_link`, `routing`; L-02, L-11…L-14 (L-07 kept only as the single-plant check, CP-07) |

*Which classification uses which structure.* Planning model: ITEM, ITEM × REGION, outgoing bridge links. Finished good / MK (line and cluster "(E)" keys included): ITEM, ITEM × PLANT, ITEM × REGION × PLANT, incoming bridge links, outgoing semi-finished, routing-option and component-option links. Semi-finished (the 3 Basic Assemblies and the 28 `-BA` keys among them): ITEM, ITEM × PLANT, incoming semi-finished links, outgoing component-option links. Component: ITEM, incoming component-option and phantom-explosion links; no ITEM × PLANT in M1 (component capacity attaches there in M2+). Phantom: ITEM only, incoming component-option or phantom-explosion links, outgoing phantom-explosion links.

*Link types.*

| Type | From → to | Weight / quantity | Other fields | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bridge | planning model → MK | weight, percent (0-100); a PM's weights sum to 100 % per month | two-axis class (B1-B12); no plant qualifier (retired in 9.9.2: alias pins and sourcing rules carry the plant) | B1-B9, B12, P7, P11 |
| semi-finished | MK → semi-finished | quantity per finished unit (> 0; 1 unless Operations says otherwise [AD]) | offset in months (2, NW-03); finishing-plant qualifier (parent plants that trigger it); supplying ITEM × PLANT row of the semi-finished item, by plant ID | B7, B11, P8, PB7 |
| routing option | MK → an ITEM × PLANT row of the same MK, named by plant ID | rate, percent, in the routing group's rate set | routing group, declared-as-component flag; never a two-step | P9, PB5, PB6 |
| component option | MK or semi-finished → component or phantom | rate (exclusive group) or attach rate, percent, from the rate set; one unit of the target per chosen unit [AD] | component group, condition on a routing option, offset | PB1-PB5, PB7, PB9 |
| phantom explosion | phantom → component or phantom | quantity per phantom unit (> 0) | offset (default 0) | PB9 |

*Planning BOM and the bridge.* Both are ITEM LINKS rows with the same dating, as-of guard, log and maintenance pattern, and the chain view (UX-04) walks them in one pass. The name **Planning BOM** stays for the configuration part: the routing-option and component-option links of one MK or semi-finished key, grouped into its routing group and component groups with rate sets per MK × region and global, plus the phantom-explosion links beneath them (NW-07). The bridge links (PM → MK) and the semi-finished links (MK → semi-finished) are not options: they carry a fixed weight or quantity per month, belong to no group and have no regional rate set [AD]. The bridge maps demand onto keys; the Planning BOM splits each key's units by configuration. Order in the explosion: bridge weight (PM → MK) → plant split, once (CP-07: override incl. alias pins, routing group, regional sourcing rule, ITEM × PLANT shares or single row) → semi-finished links at the finishing plant (offset, supplying row) → component groups at each plant → phantom pass-through.

*Phantom [gate2r16; placement AD; content U9].* A phantom item is an SAP phantom assembly: listed in the BOM, not stocked. In the explosion it has no plan row, no input, no inventory, no load, no capacity and no requirement row; the units that reach it — the load at a plant × the option's rate, or ordered units whose real configuration chose it — pass to each of its children × the phantom-explosion quantity, at the same plant and month plus any link offset, recursively through nested phantoms, so the component requirement lists only non-phantom components. Where it may appear [AD]: as the target of a component-option link under an MK or a semi-finished key, and as a child of another phantom. Never as a planning model, an MK or a semi-finished key, never as the target of a bridge, semi-finished or routing-option link, never on ITEM × PLANT or ITEM × REGION. On save, a phantom without children is rejected, and so is a **cycle of phantom-explosion links in any month in which all links of the cycle are valid** — the check runs over every combination of link rows whose validities overlap, not only over the rows valid today [critic r10]. NW-07's single level holds for planned items: a phantom is transparent, not a level. No source names a phantom (A7): the behaviour is the user's decision, which phantoms exist and their children are missing data (U9).

*Identity.* Origin + element ID: a PM and an MK with the same ID string (B1) are two items; an item folded from plant-suffixed keys has origin "folded"; components and phantoms are tool items whose ID must not equal any other item or plant ID (spec D-13). Fact keys stay as they are: `TSOP_DEMAND` is keyed by the planning-model item, `TSOP_OPS` by `manufact_key` × `plant_key`, i.e. an ITEM × PLANT row.

*Plant-suffixed keys, history and the handoff file [gate2r16; fold rule AD].* An MK ID of the form `<stem>_<plant>`, where `<plant>` resolves to a plant ID or alias (DF-04), becomes, with every other ID on the same stem, one MK item (element ID = the stem, origin "folded") with one ITEM × PLANT row per suffix plant; a stem that also exists unsuffixed as an MK ID is that same item. Each dEPM ID becomes a dated alias of type "dEPM ID" on its row. The rule is expected to match all 62 CIT keys (all of the form `CIT-nn-nn-nnn_<Plant>` [md]) and the 13 plant-specific leaves (B5) *(inference: the suffixes were not resolved against the plant list)*; how many IDs it folds and how many items result is counted in Phase 5 [critic r10]. The folded item's default plant shares come from the Confirmed history of each suffixed ID (CP-07 tier 4). **History:** fixture rows on a suffixed ID resolve to (item, suffix plant); a row whose fact plant differs from its suffix plant lands on the fact plant, flagged "suffix plant ≠ fact plant" and counted [AD]. **Handoff file (AC24):** rows keyed MK × plant are written under the alias of their ITEM × PLANT row, so dEPM receives exactly the IDs it holds today; a row on an ITEM × PLANT pair without a dEPM alias (a plant reached by override or routing, flagged "new key × plant pair") is written under the item's own dEPM ID where one exists, else listed in the handoff report as "no dEPM ID" and counted — no ID is invented [AD]; demand rows keep their planning-model IDs. Round trip: the fixtures' dEPM IDs come back unchanged in the dry-run handoff (Phase 5 exit check). **Frozen zone:** CP-08's MK × plant pair is the ITEM × PLANT row; a folded key keeps one pair per suffix plant, each with its own zone length and Working Days, as before the fold; a U4 zone entry given on a dEPM ID lands on the row its alias names. **Merged planning models [gate2r17; gate2r19; merge rule AD].** A planning-model ID `<stem>_<plant>` (the suffix resolving to a plant ID or alias) or `<stem>-BR` merges, with the stem's own ID if it exists, into one planning-model item (element ID = the stem; origin `DSALES_Product` if the stem exists, else "merged"). Each merged dEPM ID becomes a dated alias of type "dEPM ID" on **(planning model, plant)**: the suffix plant, or for `-BR` Pinecrest do Sul [gate2r19]; the stem's own ID is the alias of (planning model, every plant without its own alias). A suffix that does not resolve to a plant is not merged and is listed. **Landing:** a fixture row on a merged alias lands on the stem's cell; for Requested its units are pinned to the alias plant (CP-07 tier 1, "alias pin"); a row on the stem's own ID splits by CP-07 restricted to plants without an alias, and by CP-07 unrestricted when every plant row of the MK carries an alias (B6) or when the stem's own ID has Confirmed units at an alias plant in the 12 closed months used for the CP-07 share seed (B7) [AD, critic r12]; an alias pin fixes the plant share its rows landed with and is then an ordinary share under CP-08: an edit of the cell total spreads over the open shares in proportion to their kept basis, the pinned share included, frozen shares never move, and only an explicit plant change by the planner moves units off the alias plant — an edit never re-splits the cell by CP-07 [AD, critic r12]; pins exist only on imported fixture cycles: in the pilot each cycle's import brings its own, while a live cycle opened from the published one (PR-01) has no pins and splits by CP-07 from tier 2 on [AD, critic r12]; Sales / Invoices, Order Intake, Order Book, actuals and openings carry no plant and sum onto the cell. **Alias split [AD]:** per measure the cell keeps the per-alias split of what landed; the handoff writes each dEPM ID its kept part while the cell is unedited; once edited, Requested is written from the tool's current plant split — each plant's units under that plant's alias, the rest under the stem's ID — and the other measures in the same proportions, so the plant-encoding IDs never disagree with the tool's split [critic r12]; a cell created in the tool writes Requested by plant (each plant's units under that plant's alias, the rest under the stem's ID) and its other measures in the same proportions; where the stem has no own dEPM ID and only one alias exists, everything goes under that alias; units with no dEPM ID are listed "no dEPM ID" — no ID is invented. Round trip: an unedited fixture cell returns under exactly the IDs and values it landed with. **ITEM × REGION** of a merged item = the union of the merged IDs' rows, SOP the earliest, EOP the latest, differences listed [AD]. **FMS** of merged aliases is not additive: they are left out of the stored-FMS reproduction and listed. The merged IDs' bridge links collapse into the stem's link. **`-BR` merged [gate2r19; reverses the 9.9.2 AD]:** the `-BR` planning models lose the suffix and merge into their base planning model; the 13 `-BR` MK IDs [md] fold into the base MK as aliases of (base MK, Pinecrest do Sul) *(inference from "they lose the suffix")*, so no bridge has to switch MK by region: LATAM demand reaches Pinecrest do Sul through the seeded LATAM rule (NW-08). Phase 5 lists `-BR` MKs whose base MK does not resolve, with volume, for the data manager. **`-BA` kept [gate2r19]:** see the next paragraph.

*`-BA` two-step [critic r10; AD orchestrator, gate2r16; gate2r18 MD-Q-20; merge gate2r17 + AD].* One carrier only: the semi-finished link (NW-03). The 28 `-BA` IDs of the MK dimension [md] are seeded as semi-finished items (DF-04) *(inference, checked in Phase 5 [critic r11])*. **[gate2r19 OQ-33 d; reverses the 9.9.2 merge]** A `-BA` planning model (21, all NAM, no supply rows in the plan export [vault]) **stays a planning model of its own, sourced at Northfield**; the base planning model's NAM demand goes to Niagara by the seeded NAM rule (NW-08); Northfield and Niagara are twin plants and the order book re-splits between them. *(Reading to confirm, OQ-35: the semi-finished link below stays for the base MK finished at Niagara.)* The text below, written for the 9.9.2 merge, holds for the base MK only: the stem planning model bridges to its **base MK** (the `-BA` suffix removed, exact element-ID match [AD]); the base MK carries a semi-finished link to its `-BA` item, offset 2, quantity 1, qualified on the finishing plant, supplied by the basic-assembly plant row (Northfield). History on the `-BA` ID stays on the `-BA` planning model at Northfield [gate2r19]; the base planning model's NAM units go to Niagara by the seeded NAM rule (NW-08), so the load of the base MK at Niagara books the basic assembly at Northfield two months earlier, and load of the same MK built entirely at Northfield books none. If the base MK has no Niagara ITEM × PLANT row, the pinned or sourced Niagara load creates the pair "new key × plant pair", accepted and flagged (AC5; Phase 6 check [critic r11]). **What the evidence does not give:** which of the 28 `-BA` MK IDs pairs with which of the 21 planning models, whether each stem planning model and base MK exist, the plants of the `-BA` keys, whether the 3 Basic Assemblies (B11) are among the 28 [md; vault; md-spec; critic r11]. **Phase 5 counts and lists:** `-BA` planning models with and without a resolving stem planning model; base MKs that resolve vs not (demand-only, B9, with volume); `-BA` semi-finished items with and without an incoming link (supply-only, B9, outside DF-09 like B11); the ITEM × PLANT rows of each `-BA` key from Confirmed history; the 3 Basic Assemblies against the 28; any `-BA` ID that the fold rule also matches.

**T13 Regional sourcing rule (NW-08) [gate2r17; design AD].**

| Field | Meaning | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| key | MK × region (L-2) × plant | the plant should be an ITEM × PLANT row of the MK; if not, accepted and flagged "new key × plant pair" |
| allowed | the row exists in the month | other plants are "outside sourcing rule" for that region |
| priority | 1 = preferred | one preferred per MK × region × month |
| default share | percent, optional | if any row carries one, the rows sum to 100 %; else the preferred plant takes 100 % |
| valid from / to | by month | dated, history kept, logged; maintained by the global supply planner and the data manager |

*Walk (constructed, plant names generic).* MK X, ITEM × PLANT rows at plant L (LATAM), E (EMEA), N (NAM). LATAM rule: L priority 1, E priority 2, no shares. APAC rule: N 50 %, E 30 %, L 20 %, N preferred. EMEA has no rule; X's item-wide shares are E 70 %, N 30 %. — A LATAM cell of 100 units loads 100 at L; an EMEA cell of 100 loads 70 at E and 30 at N (tier 4); an APAC cell of 100 loads 50 / 30 / 20. A planner moves 20 LATAM units to E: allowed, no flag; to N: accepted, flagged "outside sourcing rule", on the agenda. L's zone covers month m: the LATAM cell's L share is the Operations Orderbook at (X, L) allocated to the cells whose split reaches L; a LATAM rule change dated m does not move that frozen share. In m+4 L breaches capacity: NW-05 offers the excess on E (priority 2) if E has free capacity, applied only if accepted. A confirmation at (X, L) back-allocates to the LATAM and APAC cells in proportion to their requests at L, never to EMEA cells. If X has a routing group with a rate in force for APAC, the routing rates place APAC's units and the rule only flags options outside {N, E, L}.

*Seed.* Only where history names a plant on the demand side (merged aliases, 12 closed months of Requested); all other MK × region pairs start without a rule and follow the item-wide shares (risk 20).

## Every case resolved against the to-be

| Cases | Resolution | Req / Phase |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | Edit at input grain in a scenario, within the author's write scope and the open months; DAG propagates | SC-01, CP-01, UX-03 / 5, 8 |
| S2 | Requested load at MK × plant × region; gap vs C at MK × plant; regional gap via the edge; the ops mask shows only the latest confirmed Requested; alert to ops on confirm | CP-01, CP-06, DF-10, PR-06 / 5, 6, 8, 9 |
| S3 | Proportional spread; zero aggregate rejected; Requested spread only over open shares | SC-02, CP-08 / 7, 8 |
| S4 | Cell created (5), confirmed into the common plan with provenance (8) | SC-03 / 5, 8 |
| S5 | One parent pointer of one type | SC-05 / 8 |
| S6 | Common plan moved by facts (actuals, openings, either order book) or by a change confirmed elsewhere (other role) → conflict detection, rebase | SC-06, PR-02 / 8, 9 (U1 for mid-month) |
| S7 | Personal scenarios (author only), compare, shared for review (reviewers and scoped viewers), confirmed into the common plan; candidates decided at the monthly review | SC-04, SC-05, SC-07, PR-01, UX-03 / 5, 8, 9 |
| S8 | Monthly publish = tool-side versions `CYCLE MM YYYY R` / `C`, own identity beside the loaded references of the same name (schema and lookups on identity, Phase 5); re-publish supersedes, logged; dEPM file under the official names; no dEPM / DWH `version_name` rewritten; in-place in M2 | SC-07, DF-03, RG-01, SC-05 / 5, 9 |
| S9 | Order Intake forecast entered in the sales planning mask at LE × region × PM × month over the two-FY horizon, into the author's scenario; an entry at the level the data manager set (product line by default; product family = Product Class (106 values) [gate2r19 OQ-31]; or PM) spread on current values, or on the plan Sales / Invoices when all zero; compared and published, written to the handoff on the "(E)" member; fixtures from stored `OrderIntake_Units` ("(E)" rows spread, leaf rows mapped to PM); no inventory or load consequence | DF-10, SC-02, UX-01 / 5, 12 |
| S10 | Plant filter and sort in the sales mask through PM → bridge links → the split (alias pins, sourcing rule of the cell's region, ITEM × PLANT shares); a PM at two plants appears under each with its CP-08 share, the plant rows plus "(unassigned plant)" summing to the cell; the region's other allowed plants shown at 0 %; cluster filter on the planning model's Assembly Cluster [gate2r17], "(no cluster)" where empty; Sales / Invoices and Order Intake read-only under the filter | UX-05, DF-10, DF-12, NW-01, NW-08, CP-08 / 6 (CLI), 12 (app) |
| S11 | The data manager (plants, shares and sourcing with the global supply planner) prepares a proposed bundle — ITEM, ITEM × REGION with SOP, bridge, ITEM × PLANT, sourcing, optional Planning BOM —, validated but not read by the engine; the Portfolio Review approves it; from its valid-from month the PM's cells appear in the sales mask for its regions from SOP on and its load flows; a rejection is logged | PR-04, DF-11, DF-12, NW-08 / 9 (engine), 12 (masks) |
| N1 | Opening landed through the opening-inventory mask or any DF-10 channel at LE × region × PM (fixtures at PM; negative fixture Financial accepted and flagged): Financial, IC (any sign), Total = Financial + IC; calculation opening = Total + FGNI; a coarser channel 2-3 arrival spread on the cycle's plan Sales / Invoices m..m+2 (fallback Requested); required, logged; switch requested (5; confirmed views fall back to requested, labelled) / confirmed (6) | CP-02, DF-10 / 5, 6 |
| N2 | Group recomputation (FMS by the CP-03 formula on group sums) or `not_comparable` | SC-04, CP-04, CP-03 / 5 |
| N3 | FMS node at LE × region × PM × month with dEPM's formula; "-", product-line and scope roll-ups by the same formula on sums; window ends at the next FY end, "not covered" flagged; requested in 5, confirmed in 6; no target | CP-03 / 5, 6 |
| N4 | FGNI entered by the plant's ops planner at plant × destination LE × region × PM (5), or mapped from plant × MK through the bridge (6); summed into the opening; IC excludes plant stock [gate2r13], explained by the S-06 pop-up (12); unreported plants flagged on the agenda; DF-09 Δ inventory includes it | CP-02, DF-09, NW-01, PR-05, UX-01 / 5, 6, 9, 12 |
